### MF-LLM: Simulating Collective Decision Dynamics via a Mean-Field Large Language Model Framework
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21582v1)
- **Authors**: Qirui Mi, Mengyue Yang, Xiangning Yu, Zhiyu Zhao, Cheng Deng, Bo An, Haifeng Zhang, Xu Chen, Jun Wang
- **Abstract**: Simulating collective decision-making involves more than aggregating individual behaviors; it arises from dynamic interactions among individuals. While large language models (LLMs) show promise for social simulation, existing approaches often exhibit deviations from real-world data. To address this gap, we propose the Mean-Field LLM (MF-LLM) framework, which explicitly models the feedback loop between micro-level decisions and macro-level population. MF-LLM alternates between two models: a policy model that generates individual actions based on personal states and group-level information, and a mean field model that updates the population distribution from the latest individual decisions. Together, they produce rollouts that simulate the evolving trajectories of collective decision-making. To better match real-world data, we introduce IB-Tune, a fine-tuning method for LLMs grounded in the information bottleneck principle, which maximizes the relevance of population distributions to future actions while minimizing redundancy with historical data. We evaluate MF-LLM on a real-world social dataset, where it reduces KL divergence to human population distributions by 47 percent over non-mean-field baselines, and enables accurate trend forecasting and intervention planning. It generalizes across seven domains and four LLM backbones, providing a scalable foundation for high-fidelity social simulation.
- **Summary**: Here is a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "MF-LLM: Simulating Collective Decision Dynamics via a Mean-Field Large Language Model Framework":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MF-LLM, a framework for simulating collective decision-making dynamics in large populations.  Unlike existing LLM-based social simulation approaches that often treat interactions as static or rely on handcrafted heuristics, MF-LLM explicitly models the feedback loop between individual decisions (micro-level) and population-level trends (macro-level). It consists of two main modules: a policy model (LLM) that generates individual actions based on personal states and group-level information and a mean-field model (LLM) that updates the population distribution from the latest individual decisions.  To improve simulation fidelity, the authors propose IB-Tune, a fine-tuning method based on the Information Bottleneck principle, which optimizes the mean-field model to preserve only relevant population features. The framework is evaluated on a real-world social dataset (WEIBO), showing improved alignment with real-world trends compared to baselines. The paper also explores applications like trend forecasting and intervention planning.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The novelty lies in the explicit modeling of the micro-macro feedback loop within an LLM framework for social simulation.  The use of a mean-field approximation, while established in other fields, is a valuable contribution to LLM-based simulation for scalability. IB-Tune, while based on the Information Bottleneck principle, is novel in its application to fine-tuning both the policy and mean-field modules within the LLM framework. The way this fine-tuning is realized (specific objectives and how relevant representations are extracted/compressed) adds to the novelty.

*   **Significance:**  The paper addresses a significant gap in the field of social simulation: the lack of realistic and scalable methods for capturing dynamic collective behavior. The results on the WEIBO dataset demonstrate the potential of MF-LLM to improve the fidelity of social simulations and enable more accurate forecasting and intervention planning.  The generalizability across multiple domains and LLM backbones is also a strength, indicating the robustness of the framework. A key significance lies in moving beyond simple aggregation or handcrafted rules toward a data-driven and scalable approach to social simulation.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Principled approach to modeling micro-macro feedback.
    *   The IB-Tune fine-tuning method is well-motivated and effective.
    *   Extensive experimental evaluation with real-world data.
    *   Demonstrated generalizability across domains and LLMs.
    *   Exploration of practical applications like forecasting and intervention.
    *   Clear writing and well-organized structure.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the WEIBO dataset is a good starting point, more diverse and challenging social simulation scenarios could further strengthen the evaluation. The specific details and sensitivity to parameter changes regarding the twarmup parameter and initial values should be further analyzed.
    *   The use of LLMs for both the policy and mean-field models can be computationally expensive. Exploring more efficient alternatives for the mean-field model (e.g., lightweight neural networks) could enhance scalability.
    *   The impact of exogenous signals is interesting, but the method for injecting these signals seems somewhat ad hoc. A more systematic approach to incorporating external factors would improve the framework's generality.

*   **Potential Influence:**  MF-LLM has the potential to significantly influence the field of social simulation by providing a more realistic, scalable, and data-driven approach. It could be used for a wide range of applications, including policy evaluation, public opinion analysis, and crisis management.  It is also likely to inspire further research on the integration of LLMs and mean-field methods for modeling complex social systems. The open availability of the code is also a strength in terms of influence and further development by the community.

**Score: 8**

**Rationale:**  The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of social simulation. The explicit modeling of micro-macro feedback using a mean-field LLM framework and the IB-Tune fine-tuning method addresses a crucial gap in existing LLM-based approaches. While there are some weaknesses, the strengths of the paper, including the extensive experimental evaluation and demonstrated generalizability, outweigh these limitations. The demonstrated practical applications further highlight the potential influence of MF-LLM on the field. The paper is innovative and impactful, warranting a score of 8.

- **Classification**: cs.MA
- **Score**: 8/10

### Leveraging Pre-trained Large Language Models with Refined Prompting for Online Task and Motion Planning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21596v1)
- **Authors**: Huihui Guo, Huilong Pi, Yunchuan Qin, Zhuo Tang, Kenli Li
- **Abstract**: With the rapid advancement of artificial intelligence, there is an increasing demand for intelligent robots capable of assisting humans in daily tasks and performing complex operations. Such robots not only require task planning capabilities but must also execute tasks with stability and robustness. In this paper, we present a closed-loop task planning and acting system, LLM-PAS, which is assisted by a pre-trained Large Language Model (LLM). While LLM-PAS plans long-horizon tasks in a manner similar to traditional task and motion planners, it also emphasizes the execution phase of the task. By transferring part of the constraint-checking process from the planning phase to the execution phase, LLM-PAS enables exploration of the constraint space and delivers more accurate feedback on environmental anomalies during execution. The reasoning capabilities of the LLM allow it to handle anomalies that cannot be addressed by the robust executor. To further enhance the system's ability to assist the planner during replanning, we propose the First Look Prompting (FLP) method, which induces LLM to generate effective PDDL goals. Through comparative prompting experiments and systematic experiments, we demonstrate the effectiveness and robustness of LLM-PAS in handling anomalous conditions during task execution.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents LLM-PAS, a closed-loop task planning and acting system that leverages pre-trained Large Language Models (LLMs) to improve robot autonomy in dynamic environments. LLM-PAS combines traditional task and motion planning (TAMP) with the reasoning abilities of LLMs, shifting some constraint checking from the planning phase to the execution phase. This allows the system to explore the constraint space and provide more accurate feedback on environmental anomalies. A novel "First Look Prompting" (FLP) method is introduced to guide the LLM in generating effective PDDL goals for replanning, enhancing the system's ability to recover from unexpected situations.  The paper demonstrates the effectiveness of LLM-PAS through simulation and real-world experiments, showing its ability to handle common anomalies and successfully complete tasks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Integration of LLMs with TAMP:** The core strength is the effective integration of LLMs into a traditional TAMP framework. It bridges the gap between high-level reasoning (LLMs) and low-level geometric constraints (TAMP), which is a critical area in robotics.
    *   **FLP Method:** The FLP method is a novel and well-motivated approach to prompt engineering for LLMs in robotics. By focusing on anomaly-related information and providing a preliminary solution, it enhances the LLM's ability to generate useful PDDL goals. This addresses a known weakness of directly applying LLMs to complex planning tasks.
    *   **Constraint Space Exploration:** The approach of shifting some constraint checking to the execution phase allows for more robust exploration of the constraint space. This is particularly relevant in real-world environments where complete prior knowledge is often unavailable.
    *   **Experimental Validation:** The paper presents a good range of experiments, including comparisons with existing methods, ablation studies, and real-world demonstrations. These experiments provide evidence of the effectiveness and robustness of LLM-PAS. The inclusion of Gazebo simulations in addition to Pybullet (as opposed to prior PDDLstream work) is a step towards better emulation of real world conditions for robotic simulation.
    *   **Clear problem definition**: The paper defines specific, well motivated anomalies for which this type of solution is well suited.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope of Anomalies:** While the paper demonstrates success in handling specific types of anomalies (object loss, action blocking, state change), it is unclear how well LLM-PAS would generalize to other, more complex or unforeseen situations. The experiments focus primarily on scenarios the authors predefined for specific anomalies in mind.
    *   **Reliance on LLM Quality:** The performance of LLM-PAS is inherently dependent on the capabilities of the underlying LLM. Issues like syntax errors in generated PDDL goals, as mentioned in the paper, highlight this dependency. The paper does not explore methods for mitigating these issues or improving the reliability of the LLM's output beyond the FLP method.
    *   **Integration Complexity**: The paper's solution requires a number of complex integrations for a traditional TAMP based application. The solution depends on Boost, a C++ interface, a behavior tree executor, and the LLM framework itself. This makes the problem harder for other labs to integrate the same solution given the level of expertise needed to do so.
    *   **Scalability and Computational Cost:** The paper lacks a thorough discussion on the scalability and computational cost of LLM-PAS, particularly as the complexity of the task and the number of replanning iterations increase. Using an LLM for replanning is often slower than alternatives, particularly on edge devices. This weakness should be addressed in future work.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    *   The paper's novelty lies in its specific combination of LLMs, TAMP, and a tailored prompting strategy (FLP) for anomaly handling. While individual components have been explored before, the integrated system and the focus on execution-time constraint checking are novel contributions.
    *   The significance of the paper is in demonstrating a practical approach to enhance robot autonomy in dynamic environments. By leveraging LLMs for replanning and anomaly recovery, LLM-PAS offers a promising path towards more robust and adaptable robotic systems.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a significant contribution by presenting a practical and well-validated system for integrating LLMs into TAMP. The FLP method is a valuable addition to the field of prompt engineering for robotics. However, the paper also has limitations, particularly in the scope of anomalies addressed and the reliance on LLM quality. Additionally, scalability may be a challenge to this solution.

Considering these factors, a score of **7** is justified. The paper introduces a novel and promising approach with strong experimental validation. However, the limitations in generalizability, reliance on LLM performance, and the lack of scalability analysis prevent it from reaching a higher score. Further work addressing these limitations could significantly increase the impact and significance of this research.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.RO
- **Score**: 7/10

### RDF-Based Structured Quality Assessment Representation of Multilingual LLM Evaluations
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21605v1)
- **Authors**: Jonas Gwozdz, Andreas Both
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) increasingly serve as knowledge interfaces, yet systematically assessing their reliability with conflicting information remains difficult. We propose an RDF-based framework to assess multilingual LLM quality, focusing on knowledge conflicts. Our approach captures model responses across four distinct context conditions (complete, incomplete, conflicting, and no-context information) in German and English. This structured representation enables the comprehensive analysis of knowledge leakage-where models favor training data over provided context-error detection, and multilingual consistency. We demonstrate the framework through a fire safety domain experiment, revealing critical patterns in context prioritization and language-specific performance, and demonstrating that our vocabulary was sufficient to express every assessment facet encountered in the 28-question study.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes an RDF-based framework for structured representation of LLM quality assessments, specifically targeting multilingual evaluations and knowledge conflicts.  The framework assesses LLM responses under four context conditions: complete, incomplete, conflicting, and no-context, in both German and English. It aims to capture and analyze knowledge leakage, error detection, and multilingual consistency. The authors demonstrate the framework's effectiveness through a fire safety domain experiment using GPT-4o-mini and Gemini-2.0-Flash, revealing patterns in context prioritization and language-specific performance. The RDF vocabulary is designed to align with FAIR principles.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Addressing a Gap:** The paper tackles a significant issue: the lack of standardized, structured representations for LLM evaluations, particularly in multilingual settings and when dealing with knowledge conflicts. Current evaluation approaches often lack the granularity and interoperability needed for systematic analysis. The paper proposes a concrete solution using RDF.
    *   **Multilingual Focus:** Explicitly addressing multilingual aspects is valuable. LLM performance often varies across languages, and this framework provides a structure to capture and analyze these differences.
    *   **Contextual Variations:** The consideration of various context conditions (complete, incomplete, conflicting) is crucial. It goes beyond simple accuracy metrics and examines how LLMs handle uncertainty and contradictions.
    *   **FAIR Principles:** Emphasizing FAIR principles is a strong point.  Using RDF and aligning with established ontologies promotes findability, accessibility, interoperability, and reusability of the evaluation data.
    *   **Practical Experiment:** The fire safety domain experiment provides a concrete application and demonstrates the framework's utility. It identifies specific behaviors of the tested LLMs.
    * SPARQL queries enable thorough analysis of generated models and prompt design.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Model Scope:** The empirical evaluation is restricted to two specific models (GPT-4o-mini and Gemini-2.0-Flash) and a single domain (fire safety).  Generalizability might be limited. It would be stronger with a broader range of models and potentially other safety-critical domains.
    *   **Limited Linguistic Diversity:** While the paper considers two languages (English and German), expanding to more languages, especially low-resource languages, would increase the value and applicability of the framework.
    *   **RDF Overhead:** While RDF provides structure and interoperability, it introduces a level of complexity.  The paper doesn't fully address the practical challenges of adopting this framework, such as the expertise required to create and query the RDF data.
    *   **Manual Validation:** Although the RDF structure facilitates analysis, the paper implies that the "validation assessed correctness" is done manually. Scaling this manual validation process to larger datasets or more complex scenarios could be a bottleneck. Automation options could be explored in greater detail.

*   **Novelty:**

The novelty lies in the combination of several aspects:  (1) the comprehensive RDF vocabulary specifically designed for LLM evaluation, (2) the systematic methodology for testing LLMs under varied context conditions, and (3) the focus on multilingualism and knowledge conflicts.  While individual components might exist (e.g., RDF vocabularies for other purposes, LLM evaluation metrics), the integrated approach is novel.

*   **Significance:**

The significance stems from the potential to improve LLM evaluation practices. The structured representation allows for more rigorous and reproducible analysis, fostering transparency and comparability. The insights gained from analyzing knowledge leakage and multilingual behavior can inform model development and prompt engineering. The FAIR-aligned approach promotes wider adoption and reuse of evaluation data.

**Overall Assessment:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution by providing a structured and FAIR-aligned framework for LLM evaluation.  While the empirical evaluation is somewhat limited in scope, the core concepts and methodology are sound and promising. The focus on multilingualism and knowledge conflicts is particularly relevant.  The use of RDF adds complexity, but the benefits of interoperability and queryability outweigh the drawbacks.

**Score: 7**

**Justification:**

A score of 7 reflects the paper's valuable contribution and potential impact while acknowledging its limitations. The paper effectively addresses a real gap in LLM evaluation practices by introducing a structured and FAIR-aligned framework, but the limited scope of the empirical evaluation restricts its immediate generalizability. Future work expanding the model range, linguistic diversity, and potentially automating the validation process would further strengthen the contribution. The RDF-based approach provides significant benefits for interoperability and reusability. While the overhead of RDF adoption is notable, the benefits of the structured model surpass the disadvantages.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Meeseeks: An Iterative Benchmark Evaluating LLMs Multi-Turn Instruction-Following Ability
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21625v1)
- **Authors**: Jiaming Wang
- **Abstract**: The ability to follow instructions accurately is fundamental for Large Language Models (LLMs) to serve as reliable agents in real-world applications. While existing instruction-following benchmarks are either single-turn or introduce new requirements in each turn without allowing self-correction, Meeseeks simulates realistic human-LLM interactions through an iterative feedback process. This design enables models to self-correct based on specific requirement failures, better reflecting real-world user-end usage patterns. The benchmark implements a comprehensive evaluation system with 38 capability tags organized across three dimensions: Intent Recognition, Granular Content Validation, and Output Structure Validation. Through rigorous evaluation across LLMs, Meeseeks provides valuable insights into LLMs' instruction-following capabilities in practical applications.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Meeseeks: An Iterative Benchmark Evaluating LLMs Multi-Turn Instruction-Following Ability":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Meeseeks, a novel multi-turn benchmark for evaluating the instruction-following capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs). Unlike existing benchmarks that focus on single-turn interactions or introduce new requirements in each turn without self-correction, Meeseeks simulates realistic human-LLM interactions through an iterative feedback process. This design allows models to self-correct based on specific requirement failures, mirroring real-world user-end usage patterns. The benchmark implements a comprehensive evaluation system with 38 capability tags organized across three dimensions: Intent Recognition, Granular Content Validation, and Output Structure Validation. The paper also introduces a smarter rule-augmented LLM-based evaluation to improve efficiency and accuracy in multi-turn evaluations. Through rigorous evaluation across LLMs, Meeseeks provides valuable insights into LLMs' instruction-following capabilities in practical applications.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the multi-turn iterative evaluation framework that simulates real-world human-LLM interactions more realistically than existing benchmarks. The focus on self-correction based on specific feedback is a significant departure from single-turn evaluations and those that introduce new requirements at each turn. The 38-tag capability taxonomy and the cognitive-inspired evaluation system also contribute to the paper's novelty. The smarter rule-augmented LLM-based evaluation enhances the efficiency and accuracy of the multi-turn evaluation process, addressing the cost associated with scaling single-round to multi-round evaluation.

*   **Significance:** The benchmark addresses a gap in evaluating LLMs' ability to adapt and correct their responses based on user feedback, a crucial aspect of their usability in real-world applications. By providing a more realistic evaluation environment, Meeseeks can lead to the development of more robust and user-friendly LLMs. The detailed analysis of different capability tags and the comparison of reasoning and non-reasoning models offer insights into the strengths and weaknesses of current LLMs.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Realistic Evaluation:** The iterative multi-turn framework simulates human-LLM interaction closely.
    *   **Comprehensive Taxonomy:** The 38 capability tags offer a fine-grained evaluation of instruction-following abilities.
    *   **Efficient Evaluation:** The smarter rule-augmented LLM-based evaluation reduces the cost of multi-turn evaluations.
    *   **Open-Source and Reproducible:** The authors describe ways that Meeseeks can be reproduced, and they actively maintain the project.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scale:** Although the paper mentions a 700+ entry dataset, more information on the nature of these datasets, quality control, and difficulty are needed.
    *   **Overfitting concerns** The details around the risks and measures taken to mitigate it is still concerning due to the limited scope.

*   **Potential Influence:** Meeseeks has the potential to become a standard benchmark for evaluating LLMs' interactive instruction-following capabilities. The detailed capability taxonomy can guide developers in identifying areas for improvement in their models. The findings on the performance of reasoning and non-reasoning models can inform the design of future LLMs.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field of LLM evaluation by introducing a more realistic and comprehensive benchmark. The multi-turn framework and the detailed capability taxonomy address a gap in existing evaluation methods. While some limitations exist, the paper has the potential to influence the development of more interactive and user-friendly LLMs.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Sadeed: Advancing Arabic Diacritization Through Small Language Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21635v1)
- **Authors**: Zeina Aldallal, Sara Chrouf, Khalil Hennara, Mohamed Motaism Hamed, Muhammad Hreden, Safwan AlModhayan
- **Abstract**: Arabic text diacritization remains a persistent challenge in natural language processing due to the language's morphological richness. In this paper, we introduce Sadeed, a novel approach based on a fine-tuned decoder-only language model adapted from Kuwain 1.5B Hennara et al. [2025], a compact model originally trained on diverse Arabic corpora. Sadeed is fine-tuned on carefully curated, high-quality diacritized datasets, constructed through a rigorous data-cleaning and normalization pipeline. Despite utilizing modest computational resources, Sadeed achieves competitive results compared to proprietary large language models and outperforms traditional models trained on similar domains. Additionally, we highlight key limitations in current benchmarking practices for Arabic diacritization. To address these issues, we introduce SadeedDiac-25, a new benchmark designed to enable fairer and more comprehensive evaluation across diverse text genres and complexity levels. Together, Sadeed and SadeedDiac-25 provide a robust foundation for advancing Arabic NLP applications, including machine translation, text-to-speech, and language learning tools.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "Sadeed," a fine-tuned, decoder-only small language model (SLM) for Arabic diacritization. It addresses the persistent challenges in Arabic NLP related to diacritization, such as data scarcity, varying writing styles (Classical vs. Modern Standard Arabic), contextual dependence, and limitations in existing benchmarks.  The authors fine-tune a pre-trained model ("Kuwain") on carefully curated and cleaned diacritized datasets, resulting in competitive performance against larger proprietary models. The paper also presents a new benchmark, "SadeedDiac-25," designed to overcome the shortcomings of existing benchmarks by including a balanced representation of both Classical and Modern Standard Arabic texts and undergoing expert review. The paper analyzes existing benchmark overlaps and offers a publicly available, high-quality diacritized dataset. The authors conduct extensive evaluations and provide detailed error analyses.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Addressing a Real Problem:** Arabic diacritization is a crucial task for many downstream NLP applications. The paper tackles a recognized and persistent challenge in the field.
*   **Novel Model and Fine-tuning Approach:**  While the core architecture (decoder-only transformer) isn't necessarily novel, the adaptation of the "Kuwain" model and the specific fine-tuning methodology with language injection is a valuable contribution. Reformulating diacritization as a Question-Answering task for fine-tuning is a clever approach.
*   **Benchmark Improvement:**  "SadeedDiac-25" represents a significant improvement over existing benchmarks.  The inclusion of both Classical and Modern Standard Arabic is important. The expert review process enhances data quality, and addressing dataset contamination issues is commendable.  The careful attention to punctuation ambiguities is also a strong point.
*   **Dataset Release:**  Releasing a high-quality, cleaned diacritized dataset significantly benefits the research community.
*   **Thorough Evaluation and Error Analysis:** The paper offers a comprehensive evaluation, including comparisons with various state-of-the-art models and detailed analyses of the results and limitations, including error types.  The overlap analysis of existing datasets is a critical contribution.
*   **Focus on Compactness:** The paper's emphasis on a small language model highlights a practical concern (resource efficiency) often overlooked in favor of ever-larger models.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Novelty in Core Architecture:** The base transformer architecture isn't inherently novel. The paper relies on a pre-existing, albeit custom-trained, base model.
*   **Hallucination Issues:** While the performance is competitive, the presence of hallucinations, even after the alignment correction, is a concern. Further work needs to address this more effectively.
*   **MSA Performance:** The paper admits that performance on MSA is lower due to training data limitations. More MSA specific training data may be required.
*   **Limited Comparison Against recent SOTA:** Some recent SOTA techniques for Diacritization which address the above mentioned issues might be missed out.

**Significance and Potential Impact:**

The paper has a good potential impact on the field.  The improved benchmark and released dataset provide valuable resources for other researchers. The focus on small, efficient models is a practical consideration that could lead to wider deployment of diacritization systems. However, the model's limitations, particularly the hallucination issue and MSA performance, need to be addressed in future work. Overall, the paper makes a tangible contribution by improving benchmarks, addressing data contamination, providing open-source data, and experimenting with a new model.

**Justification for Score:**

The score reflects the paper's incremental novelty, significant practical contributions, and some noted limitations. The "Sadeed" model offers an effective and efficient method for Arabic diacritization, although the underlying architecture relies on established principles. The "SadeedDiac-25" benchmark and the cleaned Tashkeela dataset are especially valuable and will likely influence future research. The authors have also taken a commendable and responsible approach by discussing their model's limitations. It is a good contribution with strong potential for future impact.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Diffusion-based Adversarial Identity Manipulation for Facial Privacy Protection
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21646v1)
- **Authors**: Liqin Wang, Qianyue Hu, Wei Lu, Xiangyang Luo
- **Abstract**: The success of face recognition (FR) systems has led to serious privacy concerns due to potential unauthorized surveillance and user tracking on social networks. Existing methods for enhancing privacy fail to generate natural face images that can protect facial privacy. In this paper, we propose diffusion-based adversarial identity manipulation (DiffAIM) to generate natural and highly transferable adversarial faces against malicious FR systems. To be specific, we manipulate facial identity within the low-dimensional latent space of a diffusion model. This involves iteratively injecting gradient-based adversarial identity guidance during the reverse diffusion process, progressively steering the generation toward the desired adversarial faces. The guidance is optimized for identity convergence towards a target while promoting semantic divergence from the source, facilitating effective impersonation while maintaining visual naturalness. We further incorporate structure-preserving regularization to preserve facial structure consistency during manipulation. Extensive experiments on both face verification and identification tasks demonstrate that compared with the state-of-the-art, DiffAIM achieves stronger black-box attack transferability while maintaining superior visual quality. We also demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed approach for commercial FR APIs, including Face++ and Aliyun.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DiffAIM, a novel approach to generate natural and transferable adversarial faces for facial privacy protection. It leverages a pre-trained diffusion model to manipulate facial identity in the latent space, using gradient-based adversarial guidance during the reverse diffusion process. This guidance optimizes identity convergence to a target while encouraging semantic divergence from the source to maintain visual naturalness.  Structure-preserving regularization is incorporated to maintain facial structure consistency. Experiments demonstrate stronger black-box attack transferability and superior visual quality compared to state-of-the-art methods, including effectiveness against commercial FR APIs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in the specific combination of techniques it employs. While individual components like diffusion models, adversarial examples, and latent space manipulation are not entirely new, the integration of these elements with the proposed identity-sensitive timestep truncation, adversarial objective (integrating both FR-driven identity convergence and U-Net driven semantic divergence) and structure-preserving regularization is a novel contribution. The use of edit-friendly latent mapping is a helpful method to initialize the process. This holistic approach distinguishes it from existing methods focusing on pixel-level noise or makeup transfer alone. GIFT utilizes GAN-inversion, but DiffAIM achieves a more controllable process.

**Significance:**  The paper addresses a critical and growing concern: facial privacy.  With the increasing deployment of FR systems, protecting individuals from unauthorized surveillance becomes increasingly important. The work's significance stems from its ability to generate adversarial faces that are both natural-looking and effective at deceiving FR systems, including commercial APIs.  The enhanced transferability against black-box models is a significant advantage, as it tackles the more realistic scenario of unknown target FR systems. The results show significant improvements over existing state-of-the-art methods.

**Strengths:**

*   **Strong Empirical Results:** The paper provides comprehensive experimental results on various datasets and against different FR models (both open-source and commercial), demonstrating the effectiveness of the proposed method.  The quantitative metrics (ASR, PSNR, SSIM, FID) support the claims of superior performance.
*   **Well-Defined Problem & Solution:** The paper clearly defines the problem of facial privacy and articulates the shortcomings of existing solutions. The proposed DiffAIM approach is well-motivated and technically sound.
*   **Detailed Ablation Studies:**  The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the contribution of each component of DiffAIM, highlighting the importance of the proposed adversarial objective, structure-preserving regularization, and timestep truncation.
*   **Good Visual Quality:** The visual comparison of generated faces with existing methods demonstrates the superior naturalness of DiffAIM.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Computational Cost:** Diffusion models are computationally intensive. While the paper doesn't explicitly address the computational cost, it's likely higher than noise-based or even GAN-based methods. A detailed analysis of runtime would be beneficial.
*   **Parameter Sensitivity:** The effectiveness of DiffAIM may depend on the careful tuning of hyperparameters (e.g., λ for structure preservation, timestep ts). The paper provides some guidance on these parameters but a more comprehensive analysis of their sensitivity is necessary.
*   **Potential for Misuse:** As with any adversarial attack technique, DiffAIM has the potential for misuse, e.g. for malicious impersonation. The paper could discuss the ethical implications and potential safeguards.

**Potential Influence:**

DiffAIM can potentially influence future research in facial privacy protection and adversarial machine learning. It demonstrates the potential of diffusion models for generating high-quality, transferable adversarial examples.  It could also lead to the development of more robust FR systems that are less susceptible to adversarial attacks.

**Score: 8**

**Justification:** The paper makes a significant contribution to facial privacy protection by introducing DiffAIM, a novel approach that generates natural and transferable adversarial faces. The comprehensive experimental results, well-defined problem and solution, and detailed ablation studies contribute to its strong performance. However, the high computational cost, potential parameter sensitivity, and misuse are potential limitations. Overall, the strengths outweigh the weaknesses, justifying a high score. This score reflects the potential impact of the paper within the domain of adversarial machine learning and privacy, while acknowledging areas that could be improved in future work.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### HoloTime: Taming Video Diffusion Models for Panoramic 4D Scene Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21650v1)
- **Authors**: Haiyang Zhou, Wangbo Yu, Jiawen Guan, Xinhua Cheng, Yonghong Tian, Li Yuan
- **Abstract**: The rapid advancement of diffusion models holds the promise of revolutionizing the application of VR and AR technologies, which typically require scene-level 4D assets for user experience. Nonetheless, existing diffusion models predominantly concentrate on modeling static 3D scenes or object-level dynamics, constraining their capacity to provide truly immersive experiences. To address this issue, we propose HoloTime, a framework that integrates video diffusion models to generate panoramic videos from a single prompt or reference image, along with a 360-degree 4D scene reconstruction method that seamlessly transforms the generated panoramic video into 4D assets, enabling a fully immersive 4D experience for users. Specifically, to tame video diffusion models for generating high-fidelity panoramic videos, we introduce the 360World dataset, the first comprehensive collection of panoramic videos suitable for downstream 4D scene reconstruction tasks. With this curated dataset, we propose Panoramic Animator, a two-stage image-to-video diffusion model that can convert panoramic images into high-quality panoramic videos. Following this, we present Panoramic Space-Time Reconstruction, which leverages a space-time depth estimation method to transform the generated panoramic videos into 4D point clouds, enabling the optimization of a holistic 4D Gaussian Splatting representation to reconstruct spatially and temporally consistent 4D scenes. To validate the efficacy of our method, we conducted a comparative analysis with existing approaches, revealing its superiority in both panoramic video generation and 4D scene reconstruction. This demonstrates our method's capability to create more engaging and realistic immersive environments, thereby enhancing user experiences in VR and AR applications.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces HoloTime, a framework for generating immersive 360-degree 4D scenes from either user-provided or model-generated panoramic images.  HoloTime uses video diffusion models to create panoramic videos from a single image or prompt, along with a 4D scene reconstruction method to transform these videos into 4D assets suitable for VR/AR experiences. The framework incorporates a new 360World dataset of panoramic videos with corresponding prompt descriptions.  HoloTime introduces "Panoramic Animator," a two-stage image-to-video diffusion model to convert images to videos, and "Panoramic Space-Time Reconstruction," a technique leveraging space-time depth estimation to generate 4D point clouds, optimized for 4D Gaussian Splatting. The paper presents comparative results demonstrating improved performance in panoramic video generation and 4D scene reconstruction.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The novelty of the paper lies in its integration of existing diffusion models with novel components tailored specifically for generating panoramic 4D scenes. While the individual modules might be based on established techniques, their combination and adaptation for this task, particularly the two-stage "Panoramic Animator" and the "Panoramic Space-Time Reconstruction" process, represent a significant contribution. The introduction of the 360World dataset is also valuable, addressing the lack of suitable training data in this specific domain. However, it needs to be critically evaluated whether the modifications and adaptions introduced in the two-stage "Panoramic Animator" and the "Panoramic Space-Time Reconstruction" process provides meaningful improvements in comparison to directly adapting existing work to the panoramic setting.

*   **Significance:** The significance of the work stems from its potential to make VR/AR experiences more immersive. By generating dynamic 4D scenes from single images, HoloTime can reduce the creation cost and complexity of VR/AR content. The approach addresses a significant gap in current diffusion model research, which often focuses on static 3D scenes or object-level dynamics. However, the significance is tempered by the fact that 4D scene generation is still a nascent field, and the impact of this research is contingent on the wider adoption and refinement of 4D technologies. It's also important to evaluate how significantly this expands upon existing 4D generation techniques when considering the panoramic aspect. Is it a significant leap in capability or a more incremental advance?

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition: The paper clearly identifies the limitations of existing diffusion models for generating immersive VR/AR content.
    *   Comprehensive framework: HoloTime integrates several components into a functional 4D scene generation pipeline.
    *   Dataset contribution: The 360World dataset addresses the data scarcity issue in panoramic video generation.
    *   Strong experimental results: The paper presents quantitative and qualitative results demonstrating the effectiveness of the proposed approach.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Dependency on existing models: The framework relies heavily on pre-trained diffusion models and depth estimation techniques. The overall system's performance is therefore limited by the performance of these underlying models.
    *   Computational cost: 4D scene generation is computationally expensive. The paper does not extensively discuss the computational requirements and efficiency of HoloTime.
    *   Limited exploration of interaction:  The paper focuses on generation but does not adequately explore the interaction aspects within the generated 4D scene. How users would realistically interact in this 4D environment is not discussed in detail.
    *   Dataset size and scope need to be further analyzed. The size of dataset can still be considered as relatively small.

*   **Potential Influence:** HoloTime has the potential to influence future research in 4D scene generation, VR/AR content creation, and panoramic video processing. The 360World dataset and the proposed techniques can serve as a foundation for further advancements in this field.

**Rigorous Rationale for Score:**

The paper demonstrates a solid contribution to the field by addressing the specific challenge of generating panoramic 4D scenes. While the individual components of the framework are not entirely novel, their integration and adaptation for this specific problem, along with the creation of the 360World dataset, warrant a good rating. However, the reliance on existing models and the lack of deep interaction analysis limit its impact.
