### EnronQA: Towards Personalized RAG over Private Documents
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.00263v1)
- **Authors**: Michael J. Ryan, Danmei Xu, Chris Nivera, Daniel Campos
- **Abstract**: Retrieval Augmented Generation (RAG) has become one of the most popular methods for bringing knowledge-intensive context to large language models (LLM) because of its ability to bring local context at inference time without the cost or data leakage risks associated with fine-tuning. A clear separation of private information from the LLM training has made RAG the basis for many enterprise LLM workloads as it allows the company to augment LLM's understanding using customers' private documents. Despite its popularity for private documents in enterprise deployments, current RAG benchmarks for validating and optimizing RAG pipelines draw their corpora from public data such as Wikipedia or generic web pages and offer little to no personal context. Seeking to empower more personal and private RAG we release the EnronQA benchmark, a dataset of 103,638 emails with 528,304 question-answer pairs across 150 different user inboxes. EnronQA enables better benchmarking of RAG pipelines over private data and allows for experimentation on the introduction of personalized retrieval settings over realistic data. Finally, we use EnronQA to explore the tradeoff in memorization and retrieval when reasoning over private documents.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "EnronQA: Towards Personalized RAG over Private Documents":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces EnronQA, a new benchmark dataset designed to evaluate Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) systems specifically for private document retrieval scenarios. The dataset consists of over 100,000 emails and 500,000 question-answer pairs derived from the Enron email corpus, segmented across 150 distinct user inboxes. The authors argue that existing RAG benchmarks primarily use public data, lacking the personal context and privacy considerations crucial for enterprise applications. They showcase the utility of EnronQA by benchmarking various RAG pipelines and exploring the trade-off between retrieval and memorization of private knowledge in Large Language Models (LLMs). The paper details the corpus cleaning, question generation pipeline, and provides baseline performance results using different retrievers, LLMs, and RAG architectures. It also explores using LoRA adapters to memorize factual knowledge.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in the creation of a large-scale, publicly available dataset specifically tailored for personalized RAG research. While the Enron email corpus is not new, its transformation into a high-quality QA benchmark with a focus on private and personalized information retrieval represents a valuable contribution. The exploration of memorization via LoRA adapters, though not entirely novel in its methodology, gains significance within the context of private data and retrieval efficiency, offering a different perspective than traditional RAG benchmarks.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical gap in RAG research by focusing on private document retrieval, which is a prevalent use case in enterprise settings. The EnronQA benchmark enables researchers to develop and evaluate RAG pipelines that are more robust and privacy-aware. The exploration of memorization versus retrieval, especially in the context of private documents, is a significant contribution that prompts further investigation into how LLMs can efficiently and securely access and utilize private knowledge.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Dataset Size and Quality:** The large size and high quality of the EnronQA dataset, with over 500,000 QA pairs, makes it well-suited for training, fine-tuning, and benchmarking RAG pipelines. The rigorous question generation pipeline, involving multiple LLM calls and quality checks, ensures the diversity and relevance of the questions.
    *   **Focus on Personalization and Privacy:**  The segmentation of the dataset into 150 user inboxes enables researchers to investigate personalized RAG techniques and privacy considerations.
    *   **Practical Relevance:** The paper addresses a highly practical and important problem: how to effectively and securely use LLMs to access and utilize private knowledge within organizations.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limitations of the Enron Corpus:** The Enron email corpus has inherent limitations. It reflects the communication patterns and language used within a specific organization at a particular point in time. The style and content might not be representative of all private document domains.
    *   **Limited Memorization Experiments:** While the exploration of memorization using LoRA adapters is interesting, the experimental setup is relatively simple. A deeper investigation into different pretraining techniques and memorization strategies would enhance the paper's contribution.
    *   **Lack of Real-World Privacy Considerations:** The paper acknowledges privacy but doesn't offer practical techniques for privacy-preserving RAG or benchmark such metrics on EnronQA.
    *   **Question difficulty:** The pipeline optimized for single-hop question generation means that EnronQA is unsuitable for benchmarking multi-hop reasoning over private emails.

*   **Potential Influence:** The EnronQA benchmark has the potential to become a widely used resource for RAG research, particularly in the areas of personalized retrieval, privacy-preserving techniques, and knowledge management. The benchmark could inspire new research directions and lead to the development of more effective and secure RAG systems for enterprise applications.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution by providing a large, high-quality dataset specifically designed for evaluating RAG systems on private documents. The focus on personalization and the initial exploration of memorization add to its significance. While the reliance on the Enron corpus and the limitations of the memorization experiments represent weaknesses, the paper addresses a clear gap in the literature and has the potential to influence future research in the field. The benchmark could inspire new research directions and lead to the development of more effective and secure RAG systems for enterprise applications.

### Mixture of Sparse Attention: Content-Based Learnable Sparse Attention via Expert-Choice Routing
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.00315v1)
- **Authors**: Piotr Piękos, Róbert Csordás, Jürgen Schmidhuber
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in large language models highlighted the excessive quadratic cost of self-attention. Despite the significant research efforts, subquadratic attention methods still suffer from inferior performance in practice. We hypothesize that dynamic, learned content-based sparsity can lead to more efficient attention mechanisms. We present Mixture of Sparse Attention (MoSA), a novel approach inspired by Mixture of Experts (MoE) with expert choice routing. MoSA dynamically selects tokens for each attention head, allowing arbitrary sparse attention patterns. By selecting $k$ tokens from a sequence of length $T$, MoSA reduces the computational complexity of each attention head from $O(T^2)$ to $O(k^2 + T)$. This enables using more heads within the same computational budget, allowing higher specialization. We show that among the tested sparse attention variants, MoSA is the only one that can outperform the dense baseline, sometimes with up to 27% better perplexity for an identical compute budget. MoSA can also reduce the resource usage compared to dense self-attention. Despite using torch implementation without an optimized kernel, perplexity-matched MoSA models are simultaneously faster in wall-clock time, require less memory for training, and drastically reduce the size of the KV-cache compared to the dense transformer baselines.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a new sparse attention mechanism called Mixture of Sparse Attention (MoSA), inspired by Mixture of Experts (MoE). MoSA dynamically selects a subset of tokens for each attention head, enabling arbitrary sparse attention patterns. This reduces the computational complexity from O(T²) to O(k² + T) per attention head, where *k* is the number of selected tokens and *T* is the sequence length. The saved compute budget is then used to increase the number of attention heads, encouraging specialization. The authors demonstrate that MoSA outperforms dense attention and other sparse attention variants in language modeling tasks, achieving better perplexity with an identical compute budget.  The paper also highlights MoSA's efficiency benefits, including reduced memory usage during training, faster wall-clock time, and a smaller KV-cache compared to dense transformers, even without specialized CUDA kernels. The method shows improved performance on long sequence tasks compared to other tested sparse attention methods, but falls short of the dense baseline for downstream tasks in the Large model, however it still produces comparable results.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The core idea of using expert-choice routing (from MoEs) to dynamically select tokens for sparse attention is reasonably novel. The combination of ideas from MoEs with attention mechanisms has been explored before, but MoSA’s specific approach of expert-choice routing for *token selection*, rather than just selecting matrices to use, differentiates it. The idea that it uses the smaller attention matrices to increase the number of sparse attention heads is also important for understanding the results.

*   **Significance:**  The primary significance stems from the practical improvements MoSA offers: better perplexity under iso-FLOP conditions, reduced memory footprint, and faster inference times. These are important considerations for deploying large language models, especially with growing context lengths. It is very significant that the method even reduces the KV-cache size, an important consideration for LLM inference. The potential to achieve better performance and lower resource usage simultaneously makes MoSA a promising alternative to dense attention.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Performance:** The empirical results convincingly demonstrate MoSA's superiority over dense and other sparse attention methods in language modeling. The iso-FLOP experiments show a clear advantage.
    *   **Efficiency:** The paper provides clear evidence that MoSA can reduce resource usage (memory, wall-clock time, KV-cache) while matching or exceeding the performance of dense models.
    *   **Practicality:** The simplicity of the approach (PyTorch implementation without specialized kernels) makes it easier to adopt and integrate into existing transformer architectures. The increased speed of MoSA while retaining the performance of the dense baseline makes it a good alternative to the much more complex Flash Attention.
    *   **Thoroughness:** The ablation studies (e.g., analyzing the number of dense heads) provide valuable insights into MoSA's behavior and the importance of hybridization.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Downstream Task Performance:** The inconsistent performance on downstream tasks (particularly for larger models) is a concern. The over-specialization might be responsible for this, so investigating regularization techniques (such as instruction tuning) is necessary.
    *   **Complexity Overhead:** While the core MoSA mechanism is relatively simple, the routing overhead (calculating and applying the router scores) should be considered. While authors claim that it is small compared to the rest, it does not include the cost of calculating the attention that must be performed before MoSA is applied in routing transformers. The computational advantages may not be realized with much smaller sequence lengths.
    *   **Comparison to other models:** The comparison between MoSA and Routing Transformer may not be fair, since Routing Transformers requires query and key transformations before K-means which has to be performed for all attention heads. It is unclear what hyper parameters were used.
    *   **Limited Sequence Lengths:** The long sequence experiment is limited to a sequence length of 8192. It also only tests hybrid models with only 4 heads for the MoSA and Routing Transformers, which has a much greater FLOP cost for longer sequences, making the fair comparison impossible.
    *   **Limited information on the number of parameters:** The model configurations in the Appendix are not well defined, as many sparse heads were created, and thus it is unclear to what the number of parameters were set.

*   **Potential Influence:** MoSA has the potential to influence research on efficient transformer architectures and attention mechanisms. Its ability to improve performance and reduce resource usage simultaneously could lead to its adoption in various applications, particularly those involving large language models and long sequences. The use of expert-choice routing for token selection could inspire other novel approaches to sparse attention.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel and promising approach to sparse attention with demonstrated benefits in language modeling. The empirical results are convincing, and the efficiency gains are practically significant. However, the inconsistent performance on downstream tasks and some limitations in the experimental setup (particularly the limited long-sequence experiments) prevent it from achieving a higher score. While the core idea is simple, there are concerns over the implementation in routing transformers, and thus the comparison may not be fair. The concerns around large models do lower the score.

### Communication-Efficient Wireless Federated Fine-Tuning for Large-Scale AI Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.00333v1)
- **Authors**: Bumjun Kim, Wan Choi
- **Abstract**: Transformer-based large language models (LLMs) have achieved remarkable success across various tasks. Yet, fine-tuning such massive models in federated learning (FL) settings poses significant challenges due to resource constraints and communication overhead. Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA) addresses these issues by training compact, low-rank matrices instead of fully fine-tuning large models. This paper introduces a wireless federated LoRA fine-tuning framework that optimizes both learning performance and communication efficiency. We provide a novel convergence analysis, revealing how LoRA rank and covariance effects influence FL training dynamics. Leveraging these insights, we propose Sparsified Orthogonal Fine-Tuning (\textbf{SOFT}), an adaptive sparsification method that streamlines parameter updates without expensive matrix multiplications and singular value decomposition (SVD) operations. Additionally, we present a Two Stage Federated Algorithm (\textbf{TSFA}) algorithm that pre-determines key parameters offline and dynamically adjusts bandwidth and sparsification online, ensuring efficient training under latency constraints. Experiments on benchmark datasets show that our approach achieves accuracy comparable to ideal scenario models while significantly reducing communication overhead. Our framework thus enables scalable, resource-efficient deployment of large models in real-world wireless FL scenarios.
- **Summary**: Here is a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a communication-efficient wireless federated learning (FL) framework for fine-tuning large language models (LLMs) using Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA). The framework addresses the challenges of resource constraints and communication overhead in FL settings by introducing Sparsified Orthogonal Fine-Tuning (SOFT), an adaptive sparsification method, and a Two-Stage Federated Algorithm (TSFA). SOFT streamlines parameter updates without expensive matrix multiplications or SVD operations by leveraging orthogonality constraints on LoRA matrices. TSFA pre-determines LoRA rank offline and dynamically adjusts other parameters online, ensuring efficient training under latency constraints. The paper provides a convergence analysis that reveals how LoRA rank and covariance effects influence FL training dynamics, and presents simulation results that demonstrate the framework's effectiveness.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper introduces several novel aspects, including:

    *   SOFT, a sparsification technique tailored for LoRA that avoids expensive SVD operations. It uses the concept of orthogonal regularization.
    *   A convergence analysis that explicitly accounts for the LoRA rank in FL.
    *   TSFA, a two-stage approach that separates LoRA rank determination from dynamic parameter adjustment.

    While these elements are new in the context of federated LoRA fine-tuning, the individual concepts such as sparsification and Lyapunov optimization have been previously explored in other domains. The application to this specific setting is where the novelty lies. The orthogonal regularization appears to be interesting and useful to avoid SVD, which is a computational bottleneck.

*   **Significance:** The significance of the paper stems from addressing a crucial challenge in deploying large models in practical FL scenarios, where resources are limited. Reducing communication costs and computational requirements while maintaining performance is a critical problem. The specific focus on wireless FL is also relevant given the increasing reliance on wireless networks.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Comprehensive approach: The paper tackles both communication efficiency and learning performance.
    *   Theoretical grounding: The convergence analysis provides insights into the interplay between LoRA rank, sparsification, and FL dynamics.
    *   Practical framework: TSFA provides a concrete algorithm for implementation in wireless FL settings.
    *   Empirical validation: The simulation results demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed methods.
    *   The orthogonal fine tuning method introduces a novel approach to sparsification by leveraging orthogonal regularization.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The convergence analysis relies on several assumptions (Assumptions 2-6), which may not always hold in practice.  The tightness of the upper bound in Theorem 1 is not discussed, potentially limiting its practical use.  Further discussion of those assumptions' real-world applicability would strengthen the work.  For example, it might be good to discuss the heterogeneous LoRA-rank setting and whether the orthogonality constraint limits performance when client data and/or models have different complexities.
    *   The communication model is somewhat simplified. Real-world wireless channels can be more complex and may require more sophisticated modeling.  It would also be useful to discuss the sensitivity to client dropout or stragglers.
    *   The evaluation is limited to the CIFAR-100 dataset and ViT-Base model. It would be more convincing to see results on larger datasets and more complex models.
    *   The paper lacks a thorough discussion of related work. While it cites several relevant papers, it doesn't provide a detailed comparison of the proposed method with existing approaches.
    *   The gains due to sparsification are not fully quantified. In order to do so, the communication cost needs to be carefully taken into account. This can include the number of bits needed for indexing the transmitted weights and the channel coding needed for reliable transmission.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field by providing a practical framework for deploying LLMs in resource-constrained FL environments. The SOFT sparsification technique could inspire further research on efficient parameter selection methods for LoRA. The convergence analysis could guide the development of adaptive FL algorithms that dynamically adjust parameters based on network conditions and resource availability. It provides a foundation for future work in optimizing wireless FL for LLMs.

**Score:** 7

**Justification:**

The paper presents a novel and practical framework for wireless federated LoRA fine-tuning. The combination of SOFT and TSFA addresses a relevant problem, while the convergence analysis offers theoretical insights.  However, the reliance on strong assumptions, the somewhat limited experimental evaluation, the lack of quantitative discussion of communications gains, and lack of discussion of related work lower the score. A score of 7 reflects the paper's solid contribution and good potential impact.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Quaternion Wavelet-Conditioned Diffusion Models for Image Super-Resolution
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.00334v1)
- **Authors**: Luigi Sigillo, Christian Bianchi, Danilo Comminiello
- **Abstract**: Image Super-Resolution is a fundamental problem in computer vision with broad applications spacing from medical imaging to satellite analysis. The ability to reconstruct high-resolution images from low-resolution inputs is crucial for enhancing downstream tasks such as object detection and segmentation. While deep learning has significantly advanced SR, achieving high-quality reconstructions with fine-grained details and realistic textures remains challenging, particularly at high upscaling factors. Recent approaches leveraging diffusion models have demonstrated promising results, yet they often struggle to balance perceptual quality with structural fidelity. In this work, we introduce ResQu a novel SR framework that integrates a quaternion wavelet preprocessing framework with latent diffusion models, incorporating a new quaternion wavelet- and time-aware encoder. Unlike prior methods that simply apply wavelet transforms within diffusion models, our approach enhances the conditioning process by exploiting quaternion wavelet embeddings, which are dynamically integrated at different stages of denoising. Furthermore, we also leverage the generative priors of foundation models such as Stable Diffusion. Extensive experiments on domain-specific datasets demonstrate that our method achieves outstanding SR results, outperforming in many cases existing approaches in perceptual quality and standard evaluation metrics. The code will be available after the revision process.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and a critical evaluation of the paper.

**Summary:**

The paper presents ResQu, a novel image super-resolution (SR) framework that combines quaternion wavelet preprocessing with latent diffusion models. The core innovation is the integration of a quaternion wavelet- and time-aware encoder, which leverages quaternion wavelet embeddings dynamically integrated during the denoising process. This encoder enhances the conditioning process within the latent diffusion model, allowing for finer control over SR image generation and leveraging the generative priors from foundation models like Stable Diffusion.  The paper demonstrates that ResQu achieves superior SR results compared to existing approaches on various datasets regarding perceptual quality and quantitative metrics.  Ablation studies are also performed to evaluate the impact of various architectural choices and dataset-specific scenarios. The work includes experiments and comparisons against state-of-the-art SR methods on datasets like DRealSR, RealSR, and DIV2K.  Additionally, it examines the effectiveness of the model in handling real-world degradations and its ability to generalize across diverse image domains, including maritime vessel images.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Novel Integration:** The paper's primary strength lies in its clever integration of quaternion wavelet preprocessing with latent diffusion models. This is not merely a simple combination of techniques but a carefully designed architecture that enhances the conditioning process within the diffusion model.
    *   **Encoder Design:** The quaternion wavelet- and time-aware encoder is a significant contribution. The dynamic integration of quaternion wavelet embeddings at various stages of denoising allows for finer control over image generation and preservation of fine-grained details, which is a critical aspect of high-quality SR.
    *   **Leveraging Generative Priors:** The approach effectively uses the generative priors from foundation models like Stable Diffusion, reducing the need for training from scratch and improving the model's ability to generate realistic and coherent images.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper provides a comprehensive evaluation of the proposed method. The use of various datasets (DRealSR, RealSR, DIV2K, ShipSpotting), evaluation metrics (PSNR, SSIM, LPIPS, FID), and ablation studies demonstrates the robustness and versatility of the framework.
    *   **Good Results:** The experimental results convincingly show that ResQu outperforms existing methods in terms of perceptual quality and quantitative metrics, suggesting a real advance in SR.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Dependency on Stable Diffusion:** While leveraging generative priors is a strength, it also makes the model somewhat dependent on the performance and biases of Stable Diffusion. A thorough investigation of how the choice of foundation model affects the outcome would have been valuable.
    *   **LPIPS Scores:** While the PSNR, SSIM and FID scores are generally favorable to ResQu, the LPIPS scores don't consistently outperform the baseline. This highlights a potential area for further improvement of the model to ensure that perceptual quality is more closely aligned with human perception.
    *   **Limited Novelty in Quaternion Wavelet usage:** The paper primarily uses QUAVE, which is a previously introduced method. While it is well integrated within a new model, there could be more detailed exploration of novel configurations/fine-tuning of the wavelet transformation beyond those provided by QUAVE.
    *   **Qualitative Limitations:** The text mentions challenges with generating unnatural sharpness, hinting at a tradeoff. More investigation is required on addressing this.

*   **Significance:**

    The paper's significance lies in its potential to advance image super-resolution techniques, especially in domains where fine-grained details and realistic textures are crucial. The incorporation of quaternion wavelets and leveraging generative priors represent a promising direction for SR research. The results and detailed ablation studies provide valuable insights for the SR community.

*   **Novelty:**

    The key novelties of the paper are:

    *   The *specific integration* of a quaternion wavelet preprocessing *within* a latent diffusion framework *in the way it's done* through this custom Quaternion Wavelet- and Time-Aware encoder.
    *   Fine-grained control of wavelet embeddings dynamically integrated during the denoising steps.

    While the quaternion wavelets themselves are not new, the way they are leveraged within the latent diffusion context, and especially the design of the encoder, adds a significant contribution.

**Score:** 7.5

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a strong and well-executed contribution to the field of image super-resolution. The novel integration of quaternion wavelets with latent diffusion models, especially the design of the wavelet- and time-aware encoder, offers a clear advantage in preserving fine-grained details and leveraging generative priors. The comprehensive evaluation and ablation studies convincingly demonstrate the effectiveness and robustness of the proposed framework.

However, some areas could be improved:
*   The LPIPS metrics could be improved.
*   Investigation into various foundation models/parametrisation beyond those provided by Stable Diffusion.
*   Investigating ways to alleviate sharpness (although the text mentions an increased need of robustness).
*   There could be more depth into the novel aspects on the Quaternion Wavelet pre-processing, since the work leverages on previous techniques from the same authors.

Overall, while the dependency on Stable Diffusion and the mixed LPIPS results hold it back from a higher score, the work represents a valuable advancement in SR research, warrants publication, and is likely to influence future work in the area.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### LLMPrism: Black-box Performance Diagnosis for Production LLM Training Platforms
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.00342v1)
- **Authors**: Zhihan Jiang, Rui Ren, Guangba Yu, Yulun Wu, Wenwei Gu, Yichen Li, Yujie Huang, Cong Feng, Zengyin Yang, Yongqiang Yang, Michael R. Lyu
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have brought about revolutionary changes in diverse fields, rendering LLM training of utmost importance for modern enterprises. To meet this demand, multi-tenant large-scale LLM training platforms have been built to offer LLM training services. Nevertheless, due to the complexity and synchronous nature of LLM training process, performance issues occur frequently and can result in substantial resource wastage. The limited visibility from the perspective of platform providers impedes existing profiling methods and poses challenges to the monitoring and diagnosis of the performance of LLM training jobs. For the first time, this paper proposes the utilization of underlying network flow data to reconstruct the training timelines of jobs based on the distinct characteristics in the LLM training procedure. We design LLMPrism, the first black-box performance diagnosis system for LLM training platforms. By progressively recognizing LLM training jobs, identifying their parallelism strategies, and reconstructing the training timelines, LLMPrism achieves non-intrusive, lightweight, and continuous monitoring of LLM training systems. Leveraging this monitoring capability, it further effectively diagnoses potential performance issues. Since Oct. 2024, LLMPrism has been deployed on our large-scale production Platform-X, in which the evaluations and deployment experiences demonstrate that LLMPrism can achieve accurate timeline reconstruction with an error within 0.3% and effectively diagnose various performance issues.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and a critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LLMPrism, a novel black-box performance diagnosis system for LLM training platforms. LLMPrism leverages network flow data to reconstruct training timelines and diagnose performance issues without requiring intrusive code modifications or direct access to tenant's configurations.  It works by identifying LLM training jobs, their parallelism strategies (data parallelism and pipeline parallelism), and then reconstructing the training timelines of individual GPUs based on network communication patterns. By analyzing these timelines, LLMPrism can detect performance anomalies such as slow steps, network congestion, and switch bottlenecks. The system has been deployed on a large-scale production platform, and evaluation results demonstrate its accuracy in identifying training jobs, parallelism strategies, and reconstructing training timelines, enabling it to effectively diagnose performance issues.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its non-intrusive approach to LLM training performance diagnosis. Previous profiling tools often require modifications to the training code or framework configurations, which are not always feasible or desirable in multi-tenant platforms due to privacy or compatibility issues. LLMPrism innovatively utilizes readily available network flow data to achieve comprehensive performance monitoring without intruding on the tenants' environments. The idea of reconstructing training timelines from network flows is also novel.

*   **Significance:** LLM training is computationally intensive and expensive, making performance optimization crucial. The black-box nature of many LLM training platforms limits the visibility of platform providers into tenant's configurations, making it difficult to diagnose performance problems. LLMPrism addresses this challenge by offering a practical solution for monitoring and diagnosing performance issues without requiring intrusive methods. This significantly enhances the manageability and reliability of large-scale LLM training platforms. Moreover, the identified insights into LLM training communications – spatial patterns, temporal patterns, and distinctive parallelism features – contribute to a better understanding of the LLM training process itself.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Non-Intrusiveness:** This is a major advantage, making LLMPrism practical for multi-tenant environments.
    *   **Accuracy:** The evaluation demonstrates high accuracy in job identification, parallelism strategy identification, and timeline reconstruction.
    *   **Practicality:** Deployment on a real-world production platform (Platform-X) validates the feasibility and effectiveness of the approach.
    *   **Comprehensive Diagnosis:** LLMPrism provides multiple levels of analysis (cross-step, cross-group, switch-level) for comprehensive performance issue detection.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Dependency on Network Monitoring Infrastructure:** LLMPrism relies on the availability of network flow data. If the network monitoring infrastructure is not in place or if the data is incomplete or inaccurate, the system's performance will be affected.
    *   **Limited Scope:** The current implementation primarily focuses on data parallelism and pipeline parallelism, while other parallelism strategies (e.g., tensor parallelism) that occur within a single node are not fully considered, though the authors acknowledge this. Further improvements might be needed to handle complex hybrid parallelism strategies.
    *   **Generalizability:** The experiments and case studies are based on a specific platform, Platform-X. While the authors argue for generalizability based on architectural similarities with other platforms, additional validation on different platforms would further strengthen the paper's claims.
    *   **k-σ rule anomaly detection**: The use of the simple k-σ rule for anomaly detection, while straightforward, might miss complex anomalies or trigger false positives. More sophisticated anomaly detection techniques might be beneficial.
    *   **Lack of Comparative Analysis**: The paper would be strengthened by a comparative analysis against baseline methods. Although profiling tools have intrusiveness challenges, a synthetic experiment comparing LLMPrism with state-of-the-art profiling tools could quantify its value.

*   **Potential Impact:**  LLMPrism has the potential to significantly improve the efficiency and reliability of large-scale LLM training platforms. Its non-intrusive nature and comprehensive diagnostic capabilities make it a valuable tool for platform providers to optimize resource utilization, reduce training costs, and provide better service to their tenants. The insights derived from network flow analysis can also be used to improve network infrastructure design and management for LLM training.

*   **Conclusion:** LLMPrism represents a valuable contribution to the field of LLM training platform management. Its innovative approach to performance diagnosis addresses a significant challenge in multi-tenant environments. While there are some limitations, the system's strengths and potential impact outweigh the weaknesses.

**Score: 8**

**Rationale:** The paper presents a novel and practical solution to an important problem. The non-intrusive nature of LLMPrism is a key differentiator. The evaluations are solid, and the deployment experience adds credibility. However, there's room for improvement regarding incorporating a broader range of parallelism strategies, stronger comparative analysis and potentially more robust anomaly detection, and further validation on diverse platforms.

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 8/10

### GAN-based Generator of Adversarial Attack on Intelligent End-to-End Autoencoder-based Communication System
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.00395v1)
- **Authors**: Jianyuan Chen, Lin Zhang, Zuwei Chen, Yawen Chen, Hongcheng Zhuang
- **Abstract**: Deep neural networks have been applied in wireless communications system to intelligently adapt to dynamically changing channel conditions, while the users are still under the threat of the malicious attacks due to the broadcasting property of wireless channels. However, most attack models require the knowledge of the target details, which is difficult to be implemented in real systems. Our objective is to develop an attack model with no requirement for the target information, while enhancing the block error rate. In our design, we propose a novel Generative Adversarial Networks(GANs) based attack architecture, which exploits the property of deep learning models being vulnerable to perturbations induced by dynamically changing channel conditions. In the proposed generator, the attack network is composed of convolution layer, convolution transpose layer and linear layer. Then we present the training strategy and the details of the training algorithm. Subsequently, we propose the validation strategy to evaluate the performance of the generator. Simulations are conducted and the results show that our proposed adversarial attack generator achieve better block error rate attack performance than that of benchmark schemes over Additive White Gaussian Noise (AWGN) channel, Rayleigh channel and High-Speed Railway channel.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a Generative Adversarial Network (GAN)-based adversarial attack on an intelligent end-to-end autoencoder-based communication system. The goal is to develop an attack model that doesn't require knowledge of the target system's details (black-box attack) while still effectively increasing the block error rate (BLER). The GAN is used to generate adversarial perturbations that exploit the sensitivity of deep learning models to dynamic channel conditions. The generator network consists of convolution, convolution transpose, and linear layers. The paper describes the training strategy, details of the training algorithm, and a validation strategy. The authors claim their approach achieves better BLER attack performance than benchmark schemes (AWGN, Rayleigh, High-Speed Railway channels). The authors emphasize that the input to the generator doesn't rely on the input signal, allowing for pre-evaluation, and that the dimensionality of the generator's input is lower than the original sample, reducing the likelihood of detection.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** While using GANs for adversarial attacks isn't entirely novel (as the paper itself acknowledges and references), applying it specifically to *intelligent* communication systems and focusing on a black-box scenario with an emphasis on practical constraints (like low dimensionality of the perturbation input) represents some degree of novelty. The claim that the input independence and dimensionality reduction are key contributions adds to this. The architecture of the GAN generator using a combination of convolutional and transposed convolutional layers might offer a slight edge, though the specific architecture's originality is not extensively justified. The use of Wasserstein distance as the loss function is meant to improve stability, which is a positive, although this is a well-known technique in the GAN literature.

* **Significance:** The significance is moderate. Attacks on deep learning-based communication systems are a growing concern, making this a relevant area of research. A black-box attack that doesn't require detailed knowledge of the target system and is efficient is potentially valuable. If the attack demonstrates consistently superior performance and robustness across different channel conditions as claimed, it could influence future designs of secure communication systems. However, the paper needs to convincingly demonstrate the performance improvement over existing techniques and clearly address the limitations.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Practical Focus:** The focus on a black-box attack suitable for real-world scenarios is a key strength. The attention to the dimensionality of the perturbation and its independence from the input signal are also positive practical considerations.
    * **Clear problem statement:** The vulnerability of AI-based communication system security is clearly stated.
    * **Evaluation:** The paper presents simulation results across different channel conditions (AWGN, Rayleigh, High-Speed Railway), which provides some evidence for the effectiveness of the proposed approach.
    * **Time consumption evaluation**: This is an important practical parameter when evaluating adversarial attack techniques.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited Details on Autoencoder Design:** There isn't sufficient detail given regarding the specifics of the autoencoder. The effectiveness of the attack is closely tied to the underlying vulnerabilities of the target autoencoder, which is a function of its design. More in-depth information would be useful.
    * **Incremental Improvement:** Although the results indicate superiority in attacking performance compared to other methods, a more rigorous statistical analysis comparing the results with the referenced attacks is missing. It is hard to judge the practical significance of the improvements without such a statistical evaluation.
    * **Limited Baseline Comparison:** Although the paper indicates that the attack performance is superior to other methods, there is a lack of direct comparison using identical experimental conditions.
    * **Justification of Architecture:** The paper claims a certain architecture for GAN, without a strong rationale.
    * **Covertness Demonstration is Weak:** The "covertness analysis" relies only on visual inspection of time-domain waveforms. This is insufficient. Quantifying the imperceptibility of the perturbation (e.g., using perceptual metrics or signal-to-noise ratio of the perturbation relative to the original signal) would significantly strengthen this analysis.
    * **Lack of Theoretical Analysis:** There's no formal theoretical analysis of why the proposed GAN architecture or loss function leads to better attack performance.  This makes it difficult to generalize the findings or understand the underlying mechanisms.
    * **Limited discussion of the computational complexity.**

* **Potential Influence:** The paper *could* have a moderate influence if its claims of superior performance are rigorously validated and the practicality is further substantiated. It may prompt researchers to explore GAN-based approaches for black-box adversarial attacks on communication systems.

**Score:** 5/10

**Justification:** The paper presents an interesting approach to a relevant problem. However, the lack of in-depth analysis, a weaker claim of novelty, limited evidence to the improvement of performance, and insufficient evidence for the improvement of the covertness weaken the significance of the work. The practicality is a positive aspect, but needs further justification and validation. More rigorous experimental validation and justification is needed for the paper to be considered high-impact.

- **Classification**: cs.IT
- **Score**: 5/10

### Toward Automated Regulatory Decision-Making: Trustworthy Medical Device Risk Classification with Multimodal Transformers and Self-Training
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.00422v1)
- **Authors**: Yu Han, Aaron Ceross, Jeroen H. M. Bergmann
- **Abstract**: Accurate classification of medical device risk levels is essential for regulatory oversight and clinical safety. We present a Transformer-based multimodal framework that integrates textual descriptions and visual information to predict device regulatory classification. The model incorporates a cross-attention mechanism to capture intermodal dependencies and employs a self-training strategy for improved generalization under limited supervision. Experiments on a real-world regulatory dataset demonstrate that our approach achieves up to 90.4% accuracy and 97.9% AUROC, significantly outperforming text-only (77.2%) and image-only (54.8%) baselines. Compared to standard multimodal fusion, the self-training mechanism improved SVM performance by 3.3 percentage points in accuracy (from 87.1% to 90.4%) and 1.4 points in macro-F1, suggesting that pseudo-labeling can effectively enhance generalization under limited supervision. Ablation studies further confirm the complementary benefits of both cross-modal attention and self-training.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Toward Automated Regulatory Decision-Making: Trustworthy Medical Device Risk Classification with Multimodal Transformers and Self-Training."

**Summary:**

This paper presents a multimodal deep learning framework for automating the risk classification of medical devices, a crucial task in regulatory compliance. The framework combines textual descriptions and visual information (product images) using a Transformer-based architecture with a cross-attention mechanism to capture inter-modal dependencies.  A self-training strategy is also employed to improve generalization, especially under limited supervision. The authors curate a dataset from the National Medical Products Administration (NMPA) of China, augment it with product images, and demonstrate that their approach achieves superior accuracy and AUROC compared to unimodal baselines and standard multimodal fusion techniques.  The paper also analyzes the robustness of the model to noisy inputs and modality-specific perturbations. Finally, the authors discuss the potential applications of their work in automated pre-screening, compliance verification, and regulatory harmonization efforts.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Addresses an Important Problem:** The paper tackles a real-world problem with significant implications for regulatory efficiency, clinical safety, and innovation in the medical device industry. The manual classification of medical devices is labor-intensive and prone to errors, making automation a valuable goal.
    *   **Well-Defined Methodology:** The authors present a clear and well-documented methodology, describing the data preprocessing steps, the model architecture (including the cross-modal Transformer and self-training), and the experimental setup. The inclusion of ablation studies allows for a thorough assessment of each component's contribution.
    *   **Strong Empirical Results:** The experiments demonstrate a clear improvement in performance compared to unimodal baselines and standard multimodal approaches. The self-training strategy further enhances the model's accuracy and generalization ability.
    *   **Robustness Analysis:** The evaluation of the model's robustness to noisy inputs and modality-specific perturbations is a key strength, highlighting its potential for deployment in real-world scenarios where data quality may vary.
    *   **Practical Implications:** The paper explicitly discusses the practical implications of its work, outlining potential applications in automated pre-screening, compliance verification, and regulatory harmonization.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Dataset:** The dataset is a significant limitation. It is relatively small (1000 entries) and exclusively sourced from the Chinese NMPA. This raises concerns about the generalizability of the results to other regulatory environments (e.g., the FDA or EMA) with different regulatory languages, documentation formats, and device classifications.
    *   **Lack of Structured Metadata:** The model does not incorporate structured metadata fields (e.g., intended clinical use, manufacturer identity, device composition), which are often central to regulatory assessments. The authors acknowledge this limitation, but it reduces the completeness of their model.
    *   **Reliance on Confidence in Self-Training:** The self-training strategy relies on the assumption that confidence correlates with correctness, which may not always hold true, particularly in the presence of imbalanced class distributions or evolving device categories. While the paper uses model ensembling to try and mitigate this, it remains a potential vulnerability.
    *   **Limited Novelty in Architecture:** While the application is novel, the core architectural components (Transformers, cross-attention, self-training) are not particularly groundbreaking. The paper combines existing techniques in a reasonably straightforward manner. However the domain adapation of the image and text encoders are noteworthy.

*   **Significance:**

    *   The paper represents a significant step toward automated regulatory decision-making in the medical device industry. By demonstrating the effectiveness of multimodal deep learning for risk classification, it opens up new possibilities for improving regulatory efficiency, clinical safety, and innovation.
    *   The framework is well-designed to allow it to scale, be interperable, and domain sensitive.
    *   The work has the potential to influence regulatory practices and inspire further research in this area.

**Overall Score and Justification:**

I assign a score of **7/10**.

**Rationale:**

The paper addresses a significant problem with a clear methodology and strong empirical results, which makes its contribution valuable to the field. However, the limitations of the dataset and reliance of confidence metrics in the self training significantly restricts it's novelty and generalizability. The architectural novelty is limited, however, the adaptation of transformers to both modalities within the medical device classification domain is important. The contributions have significant potential to influence regulatory practices and inspire further research in this area. Future work should focus on expanding the dataset, incorporating structured metadata, and addressing the potential vulnerabilities of the self-training strategy.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 0/10

### Leveraging Pretrained Diffusion Models for Zero-Shot Part Assembly
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.00426v1)
- **Authors**: Ruiyuan Zhang, Qi Wang, Jiaxiang Liu, Yu Zhang, Yuchi Huo, Chao Wu
- **Abstract**: 3D part assembly aims to understand part relationships and predict their 6-DoF poses to construct realistic 3D shapes, addressing the growing demand for autonomous assembly, which is crucial for robots. Existing methods mainly estimate the transformation of each part by training neural networks under supervision, which requires a substantial quantity of manually labeled data. However, the high cost of data collection and the immense variability of real-world shapes and parts make traditional methods impractical for large-scale applications. In this paper, we propose first a zero-shot part assembly method that utilizes pre-trained point cloud diffusion models as discriminators in the assembly process, guiding the manipulation of parts to form realistic shapes. Specifically, we theoretically demonstrate that utilizing a diffusion model for zero-shot part assembly can be transformed into an Iterative Closest Point (ICP) process. Then, we propose a novel pushing-away strategy to address the overlap parts, thereby further enhancing the robustness of the method. To verify our work, we conduct extensive experiments and quantitative comparisons to several strong baseline methods, demonstrating the effectiveness of the proposed approach, which even surpasses the supervised learning method. The code has been released on https://github.com/Ruiyuan-Zhang/Zero-Shot-Assembly.
- **Summary**: Here is a concise summary and a critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a zero-shot 3D part assembly method that leverages pre-trained point cloud diffusion models to guide the assembly process. The core idea is to use the diffusion model as a discriminator, iteratively refining the poses of individual parts to form realistic shapes.  It translates the use of a diffusion model to the assembly problem into an Iterative Closest Point (ICP) like process. A novel pushing-away strategy is introduced to address collisions between parts. The method is evaluated extensively, showing it outperforms several baselines, including some supervised learning approaches.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the application of pre-trained diffusion models to the zero-shot 3D part assembly problem. While diffusion models are becoming prevalent in various generative tasks, their use as a discriminator for pose refinement in assembly tasks is relatively new. Translating its use to an ICP like process provides some theoretical grounding. The pushing-away strategy, while not entirely novel in itself, is a pragmatic addition that addresses a critical practical challenge in part assembly.

*   **Significance:** The significance of the work stems from its potential to reduce the reliance on manually labeled data for 3D part assembly. The high cost of data collection has been a major bottleneck in this field, and a zero-shot approach that can leverage readily available pre-trained models represents a significant step forward. The fact that the proposed method surpasses some supervised baselines highlights its effectiveness and potential impact.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem statement and motivation.
    *   Well-defined methodology with theoretical justification.
    *   Extensive experimental evaluation against strong baselines.
    *   Practical addition of the push-away strategy for collision handling.
    *   Code is released

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The method relies on the quality of pre-trained diffusion models.  Performance is likely limited to object categories the diffusion model has been trained on.
    *   While the method outperforms certain supervised methods, it is difficult to see this scaling well with more advanced supervised methods for the task, especially if the supervised methods were trained with data that matches the distribution seen by the pre-trained diffusion model.
    *   Severe misalignment and the dependence on parts semantics are recognized failure cases, as mentioned in the paper.
    *   More detailed experiments on parts beyond chairs should be present.

*   **Impact and Influence:** The paper demonstrates the feasibility of using diffusion models for zero-shot 3D part assembly, which could spur further research in this area. It could also inspire the development of more robust and generalizable assembly methods that require minimal supervision. The paper's insights into the limitations of the approach will also be valuable for future research efforts.

*   **Score Justification:** The paper presents a novel application of diffusion models to a relevant problem, demonstrating promising results. However, the method's reliance on pre-trained models, the limitations identified in its robustness, and a not particularly detailed theoretical contribution hold it back from being a truly groundbreaking contribution. The clear presentation, thorough evaluation, and code release are definite pluses.

Score: 6.5

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### Distributed Retrieval-Augmented Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.00443v1)
- **Authors**: Chenhao Xu, Longxiang Gao, Yuan Miao, Xi Zheng
- **Abstract**: As large language models (LLMs) become increasingly adopted on edge devices, Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) is gaining prominence as a solution to address factual deficiencies and hallucinations by integrating external knowledge. However, centralized RAG architectures face significant challenges in data privacy and scalability. For instance, smart healthcare services often rely on collecting sensitive patient data and building a centralized knowledge base to provide better diagnosis and treatment advice, while privacy concerns significantly impede this process. Besides, maintaining a comprehensive and continuously updated knowledge base is costly, particularly in response to regional epidemics and rapidly mutating viruses. To address these challenges, this paper introduces Distributed Retrieval-Augmented Generation (DRAG), a novel framework that improves data privacy by eliminating the need for a centralized knowledge base and restoring data control to owners. DRAG incorporates a Topic-Aware Random Walk (TARW) algorithm that leverages LLMs to extract query topics and facilitate targeted peer discovery within a peer-to-peer network, enabling efficient knowledge retrieval in decentralized environments. Extensive experiments across three diverse datasets and LLMs demonstrate that DRAG with TARW achieves near-centralized RAG performance by using half as many messages as flooding. The code is available at https://github.com/xuchenhao001/DRAG.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary**

The paper introduces Distributed Retrieval-Augmented Generation (DRAG), a novel framework designed to enhance data privacy and scalability in Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) systems, especially in edge computing environments.  DRAG eliminates the need for a centralized knowledge base by enabling peer-to-peer knowledge retrieval and generation across a distributed network. The paper proposes a Topic-Aware Random Walk (TARW) algorithm that leverages large language models (LLMs) to extract query topics, guide peer discovery, and facilitate efficient knowledge retrieval.  The authors conduct experiments on diverse datasets (MMLU, Medical Extended, News) and using several LLMs (Llama 3.2-3B, Gemma 2 2B, Qwen2.5 3B), demonstrating that DRAG with TARW achieves performance comparable to centralized RAG while significantly reducing communication overhead.

**Critical Evaluation**

*Novelty:*  The core idea of decentralizing RAG is innovative and addresses a significant limitation of centralized RAG architectures, especially in privacy-sensitive domains. The concept of DRAG aligns with the increasing trend of edge computing and federated learning. The Topic-Aware Random Walk (TARW) algorithm is a valuable contribution as it enhances peer discovery within the distributed environment. However, federated RAG (mentioned in related works) does explore similar ideas, weakening this paper's novelty claim.

*Significance:*  The paper's findings have important implications for several application domains.  In healthcare, where patient data privacy is paramount, DRAG could facilitate knowledge sharing and collaborative query resolution without compromising data security.  Autonomous driving and other IoT applications could also benefit from DRAG's distributed architecture. The paper directly addresses and aims to mitigate the well-known hallucination problem in LLMs which makes it significant.

*Strengths:*

*   **Addresses a critical problem:**  The paper tackles the important issues of data privacy and scalability in RAG systems, which are crucial for their widespread adoption.
*   **Well-defined framework:**  DRAG provides a clear and well-defined architecture for distributed RAG.
*   **Novel algorithm:** The TARW algorithm offers a practical approach to efficient peer discovery in decentralized environments.
*   **Comprehensive experiments:**  The authors conduct extensive experiments using diverse datasets and LLMs, demonstrating the effectiveness of DRAG.
*   **Clear presentation:** The paper is well-written and easy to understand, with clear explanations of the proposed framework and algorithm.

*Weaknesses:*

*   **Complexity of deployment:** Implementing DRAG in real-world edge computing environments could be challenging due to network constraints, resource limitations, and security concerns.
*   **Reliance on network topology:** The performance of DRAG depends on the connectivity and structure of the P2P network.  Sparsely connected networks may limit its effectiveness. The paper does explore this, but more real-world network analysis would strengthen the work.
*   **Knowledge reliability:** Ensuring the reliability and consistency of knowledge distributed across multiple peers is a major challenge that requires further investigation.  While the paper mentions validation and ranking mechanisms, it doesn't delve into the complexities of implementing such mechanisms in practice.
*   **Incentive Mechanisms:** The paper mentions the need for incentive mechanisms to encourage knowledge sharing. Without a proper incentive system, the effectiveness of the model can be affected
*   **Privacy considerations:** While DRAG attempts to improve privacy, the user-filtered knowledge snippets may still lead to information leakage. A more rigorous privacy analysis would be beneficial.
*   **Limited comparison to Federated RAG:** The paper references Federated RAG approaches but doesn't sufficiently highlight the key differences and advantages of DRAG over these existing methods. The novelty of DRAG feels weaker due to this lack of a more thorough comparison.

*Potential Influence:* The DRAG framework has the potential to significantly impact the design of RAG systems for edge computing environments. By improving data privacy and scalability, DRAG could enable new applications in healthcare, autonomous driving, and other domains. Future research could build upon DRAG to develop more robust and efficient distributed RAG architectures.

**Justification for Score**

The paper presents a solid and innovative contribution to the field of Retrieval-Augmented Generation. It addresses a crucial need for data privacy and scalability in RAG systems, proposing a well-defined and experimentally validated framework. However, some weaknesses remain to be addressed, especially about real-world deployability, knowledge reliability, incentive mechanisms, and privacy analysis. Furthermore, a more thorough comparison with existing Federated RAG approaches is required to strengthen its novelty. Although those weaknesses exist, the potential impact of this paper is evident in privacy-sensitive use cases and in the domain of Edge Computing.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.DC
- **Score**: 7/10

### Data Therapist: Eliciting Domain Knowledge from Subject Matter Experts Using Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.00455v1)
- **Authors**: Sungbok Shin, Hyeon Jeon, Sanghyun Hong, Niklas Elmqvist
- **Abstract**: Effective data visualization requires not only technical proficiency but also a deep understanding of the domain-specific context in which data exists. This context often includes tacit knowledge about data provenance, quality, and intended use, which is rarely explicit in the dataset itself. We present the Data Therapist, a web-based tool that helps domain experts externalize this implicit knowledge through a mixed-initiative process combining iterative Q&A with interactive annotation. Powered by a large language model, the system analyzes user-supplied datasets, prompts users with targeted questions, and allows annotation at varying levels of granularity. The resulting structured knowledge base can inform both human and automated visualization design. We evaluated the tool in a qualitative study involving expert pairs from Molecular Biology, Accounting, Political Science, and Usable Security. The study revealed recurring patterns in how experts reason about their data and highlights areas where AI support can improve visualization design.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "Data Therapist," a web-based system designed to elicit domain knowledge from subject matter experts (SMEs) about datasets using a mixed-initiative approach.  It combines iterative question-and-answer dialogues powered by a Large Language Model (LLM) with user-driven annotation capabilities at varying levels of granularity (cells, rows, columns, overall dataset).  The system generates targeted questions based on dataset analysis and metadata frameworks, cross-references annotations, tracks coverage, and suggests areas for further exploration.  The extracted knowledge base can then inform visualization design.  The authors validated the system with a qualitative user study involving pairs of experts from Molecular Biology, Accounting, Political Science, and Usable Security.  The study compared domain knowledge elicited by Data Therapist to that extracted via a human interviewer. The findings suggest that the system can effectively capture domain-specific context and tacit knowledge, though there are differences in the types of insights elicited.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**  The idea of using an LLM to facilitate knowledge elicitation for visualization is interesting, though arguably not entirely new.  Existing works have explored LLMs for metadata extraction and generating visualizations.  The novelty lies in the specific approach of combining LLM-driven question answering with interactive annotation at different granularities *within* the context of visualization design. The focus on eliciting *tacit* knowledge, or "data hunches", is a strength. It addresses a significant gap in the current visualization design pipeline.  The idea of the Data Therapist acting as an AI assistant helping domain experts to externalize their knowledge has some novelty.

**Significance:** The paper addresses a real problem: bridging the communication gap between domain experts and visualization designers. A common issue in design studies is the knowledge gap, where it’s difficult for designers who are typically not experts in the domain to elicit all the critical, tacit information required for an efficient visualization design. Providing a structured approach to eliciting and documenting this knowledge could significantly improve the effectiveness of visualizations. The system could reduce misinterpretations of data and improve the utility of visualizations in specific domains. The emphasis on mixed-initiative interaction is also valuable; it acknowledges the need for a balance between system guidance and expert input. It also provides a framework for other researchers to build on.

**Strengths:**

*   **Well-defined problem:** Clearly identifies a key challenge in visualization design (knowledge elicitation).
*   **Mixed-initiative approach:** Combines system guidance with expert input, which is crucial for capturing tacit knowledge.
*   **Multi-granular annotation:**  The ability to annotate at different levels of detail (cells, rows, columns) is a significant advantage.
*   **Qualitative evaluation:**  The study involving experts from diverse domains provides valuable insights into the system's utility and limitations.
*   **Addresses a specific gap:** Tackles the upstream problem of creating an effective knowledge base before visual design takes place.
* The experiments are structured appropriately with one group getting help from Data Therapist and another from human interviewer.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Generalizability:** Although the study involves four domains, the results are still qualitative.  More extensive quantitative evaluations are needed to assess the generalizability of the approach across a wider range of datasets and domains.
*   **LLM Dependency:**  The system's performance is heavily dependent on the capabilities of the underlying LLM.  The choice of GPT-4 is reasonable, but further research is needed to understand how the system would perform with different LLMs or with changes in LLM behavior.
*   **Comparison to Human Interviewer:** While the study includes a comparison to a human interviewer, it might benefit from a more nuanced comparison. For example, it would be useful to explore the types of questions and insights that each approach elicits that the other doesn't. The data in Table 3 indicates that HI helped domain experts to estimate the general knowledge of a lay audience more closely, compared to DT. In contrast, the data also indicates that DT was able to elicit more domain-specific insights to HI's. More nuanced comparisons could provide more interesting data.
*   **Limited Automation on Viz Design:** The paper mainly focuses on elicitation of data, but it would be interesting to explore how the knowledge base is actually integrated into or could be leveraged into visualization systems. The paper mainly addresses the upstream aspect, but less the "downstream" part on how the tacit knowledge is actually transferred into visualizations.

**Justification for Score:**

I am assigning a score of **7**.  The paper addresses a real-world problem in visualization design in an interesting way by eliciting the tacit domain knowledge for more efficient designs. The mixed-initiative approach is significant, and the design seems carefully thought out. The study does provide data on Data Therapist's usefulness. However, limitations in the evaluation, dependence on a specific LLM, and the lack of integration into a visualization pipeline temper the overall impact.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.HC
- **Score**: 7/10

### Red Teaming Large Language Models for Healthcare
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.00467v1)
- **Authors**: Vahid Balazadeh, Michael Cooper, David Pellow, Atousa Assadi, Jennifer Bell, Jim Fackler, Gabriel Funingana, Spencer Gable-Cook, Anirudh Gangadhar, Abhishek Jaiswal, Sumanth Kaja, Christopher Khoury, Randy Lin, Kaden McKeen, Sara Naimimohasses, Khashayar Namdar, Aviraj Newatia, Allan Pang, Anshul Pattoo, Sameer Peesapati, Diana Prepelita, Bogdana Rakova, Saba Sadatamin, Rafael Schulman, Ajay Shah, Syed Azhar Shah, Syed Ahmar Shah, Babak Taati, Balagopal Unnikrishnan, Stephanie Williams, Rahul G Krishnan
- **Abstract**: We present the design process and findings of the pre-conference workshop at the Machine Learning for Healthcare Conference (2024) entitled Red Teaming Large Language Models for Healthcare, which took place on August 15, 2024. Conference participants, comprising a mix of computational and clinical expertise, attempted to discover vulnerabilities -- realistic clinical prompts for which a large language model (LLM) outputs a response that could cause clinical harm. Red-teaming with clinicians enables the identification of LLM vulnerabilities that may not be recognised by LLM developers lacking clinical expertise. We report the vulnerabilities found, categorise them, and present the results of a replication study assessing the vulnerabilities across all LLMs provided.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents the design and findings of a workshop focused on "Red Teaming Large Language Models (LLMs) for Healthcare." The workshop brought together computational and clinical experts to identify vulnerabilities in LLMs when applied to realistic healthcare scenarios. Participants devised clinical prompts and evaluated the LLM responses for potential harm. The study identifies various categories of vulnerabilities, including hallucination, image interpretation failure, anchoring bias, and incorrect medical knowledge. The authors also assessed the reproducibility of these vulnerabilities across different LLMs over time, revealing that LLM vulnerabilities are a constantly moving target and are not easily captured by static benchmarks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Real-world Focus:** The workshop approach, involving clinicians in real-world scenarios, is a significant strength. It moves beyond standardized benchmarks and explores practical clinical applications.
    *   **Vulnerability Categories:** The identified categories of vulnerabilities (hallucination, image interpretation failure, anchoring bias, etc.) provide a valuable framework for understanding potential LLM limitations in healthcare.
    *   **Replication Study:**  The longitudinal replication study highlights the dynamic nature of LLM vulnerabilities, reinforcing the need for continuous evaluation.
    *   **Clinician Involvement:** Emphasizing the importance of clinician expertise is critical, since LLM developers without clinical expertise may miss vulnerabilities.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Selection Bias:** The authors acknowledge that the preference for ChatGPT may introduce bias in the reported vulnerabilities.
    *   **Reporting Focus:**  The study primarily focused on reporting vulnerabilities rather than systematically assessing the prevalence of harmful responses, limiting the ability to quantify risk.
    *   **Specialty Representation:** The authors acknowledge some clinical specialities are not properly represented, limiting scope.
    *   **LLM Training:** Acknowledging that many LLMs have likely been trained on tokens generated from clinical processes (e.g., clinical notes, patient care guidelines, medical textbooks, etc.) could impact results and potentially limit a real-world test.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**
    *   The use of clinicians to "red team" LLMs for healthcare applications is useful, and it brings a crucial perspective to the design and safety of LLMs in this area.
    *   While previous studies have explored LLM limitations in healthcare, the study's focus on a workshop setting, emphasizing practical scenarios and categorizing vulnerabilities is unique and provides actionable insights.
    *   The demonstration that LLM vulnerabilities are not static highlights the need for continuous monitoring rather than relying on benchmarks for safety.

*   **Potential Influence:**
    *   The study's findings can inform the development of safer and more reliable LLMs for healthcare applications.
    *   The vulnerability categories can guide clinicians in identifying and mitigating potential errors when using LLMs.
    *   The replication study underscores the need for dynamic evaluation frameworks to ensure ongoing safety.

*   **Justification for Score:** While the research is valuable and addresses a crucial area, its limitations in scope, reliance on the workshop setting, and acknowledges selection bias slightly diminish its overall impact. There is some novelty in bringing clinical and computational expertise to red teaming, but the actual findings don't present breakthrough insights. It highlights potential risk but the results are not easily reproducible.
    
**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Interpretable Spatial-Temporal Fusion Transformers: Multi-Output Prediction for Parametric Dynamical Systems with Time-Varying Inputs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.00473v1)
- **Authors**: Shuwen Sun, Lihong Feng, Peter Benner
- **Abstract**: We explore the promising performance of a transformer model in predicting outputs of parametric dynamical systems with external time-varying input signals. The outputs of such systems vary not only with physical parameters but also with external time-varying input signals. Accurately catching the dynamics of such systems is challenging. We have adapted and extended an existing transformer model for single output prediction to a multiple-output transformer that is able to predict multiple output responses of these systems. The multiple-output transformer generalizes the interpretability of the original transformer. The generalized interpretable attention weight matrix explores not only the temporal correlations in the sequence, but also the interactions between the multiple outputs, providing explanation for the spatial correlation in the output domain. This multiple-output transformer accurately predicts the sequence of multiple outputs, regardless of the nonlinearity of the system and the dimensionality of the parameter space.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

This paper introduces the Interpretable Spatial-Temporal Fusion Transformer (iSTFT), an extension of the Temporal Fusion Transformer (TFT), designed for predicting the time evolution of multiple outputs (QoIs) of parametric dynamical systems influenced by time-varying input signals. The key innovation lies in the iSTFT's ability to capture not only temporal correlations within each output but also spatial correlations *between* the multiple outputs. This is achieved by reshaping the output data and introducing a block-wise masked multi-head attention mechanism, which allows for interpretable analysis of the relationships between the different output variables. The paper successfully applies iSTFT to three parametric dynamical systems: the Lorenz-63 model, the FitzHugh-Nagumo model, and a Ferrocyanide oxidation reaction model, demonstrating its ability to accurately predict multiple outputs across various parameter and time domains. The interpretability of the model is showcased through analysis of the attention weight matrices and variable importance, providing insights into the spatial-temporal relationships between the system's outputs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in extending the TFT architecture to handle multiple outputs of parametric dynamical systems while preserving and enhancing interpretability. While TFT itself is an established architecture, the reshaping of data for spatial information and, more importantly, the block-wise masking of the attention matrix to understand inter-output relationships are significant contributions. The extension allows for simultaneous prediction of multiple QOIs with a *single* trained model, which is a clear advantage over training separate TFT models for each output. The interpretability aspect, through the analysis of the attention weights and variable importance, is crucial, as it goes beyond simply achieving good prediction accuracy and provides insights into the underlying dynamics of the system. However, the core transformer architecture is not substantially modified; the contribution is mostly in how the *inputs* are encoded and the *outputs* interpreted, with significant adjustments to the attention masking strategy.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance stems from its potential impact on several areas:

    *   **Surrogate Modeling:** The iSTFT provides a powerful data-driven surrogate model for complex systems that are difficult or computationally expensive to simulate directly. This has implications for various engineering fields, where rapid evaluation of system behavior under different parameter and input conditions is critical. The ability to simultaneously predict multiple QoIs enhances its utility for multi-objective optimization and sensitivity analysis.

    *   **System Understanding:** The interpretability features of iSTFT allow for a deeper understanding of the relationships between different outputs of a dynamical system. This could lead to new insights into system behavior and inform design optimization strategies.
